2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eRedux is a JS library for predictable and maintainable global state management.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eIt helps you write applications that behave consistently, run in different environments (client, server, and native), and are easy to test. On top of that, it provides a great developer experience, such as \u003ca href=\"https://github.com/reduxjs/redux-devtools\"\u003elive code editing combined with a time traveling debugger\u003c/a\u003e.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eYou can use Redux together with \u003ca href=\"https://react.dev\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003eReact\u003c/a\u003e, or with any other view library. The Redux core is tiny (2kB, including dependencies), and has a rich ecosystem of addons.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ca href=\"https://redux-toolkit.js.org\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eRedux Toolkit\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/a\u003e is our official recommended approach for writing Redux logic. It wraps around the Redux core, and contains packages and functions that we think are essential for building a Redux app. Redux Toolkit builds in our suggested best practices, simplifies most Redux tasks, prevents common mistakes, and makes it easier to write Redux applications.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ca target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noopener noreferrer nofollow\" Reactiflux is a great place to hang out, ask questions, and learn - please come and join us there!\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch2 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003eBefore Proceeding Further\u003c/h2\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-before-proceeding-further\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Before Proceeding Further\" href=\"#before-proceeding-further\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eRedux is a valuable tool for organizing your state, but you should also consider whether it's appropriate for your situation. Please don't use Redux just because someone said you should - instead, please take some time to understand the potential benefits and tradeoffs of using it.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eHere are some suggestions on when it makes sense to use Redux:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cul dir=\"auto\"\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eYou have reasonable amounts of data changing over time\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eYou need a single source of truth for your state\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003eYou find that keeping all your state in a top-level component is no longer sufficient\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eYes, these guidelines are subjective and vague, but this is for a good reason. The point at which you should integrate Redux into your application is different for every user and different for every application.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cblockquote\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eFor more thoughts on how Redux is meant to be used, please see:\u003c/strong\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cul dir=\"auto\"\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003e\u003ca href=\"https://changelog.com/posts/when-and-when-not-to-reach-for-redux\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003eWhen (and when not) to reach for Redux\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003e\u003ca href=\"https://medium.com/@dan_abramov/you-might-not-need-redux-be46360cf367\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003eYou Might Not Need Redux\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/strong\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003e\u003ca href=\"https://blog.isquaredsoftware.com/2017/05/idiomatic-redux-tao-of-redux-part-1/\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003eThe Tao of Redux, Part 1 - Implementation and Intent\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/strong\u003e\u003cbr\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003e\u003ca href=\"https://blog.isquaredsoftware.com/2017/05/idiomatic-redux-tao-of-redux-part-2/\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003eThe Tao of Redux, Part 2 - Practice and Philosophy\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003e\u003ca href=\"https://redux.js.org/faq\" rel=\"nofollow\"\u003eRedux FAQ\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\u003c/blockquote\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"\u003e\u003ch2 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\"\u003eBasic Example\u003c/h2\u003e\u003ca id=\"user-content-basic-example\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Basic Example\" href=\"#basic-example\"\u003e\u003csvg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003cpath d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"\u003e\u003c/path\u003e\u003c/svg\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/div\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eThe whole global state of your app is stored in an object tree inside a single \u003cem\u003estore\u003c/em\u003e.\nThe only way to change the state tree is to create an \u003cem\u003eaction\u003c/em\u003e, an object describing what happened, and \u003cem\u003edispatch\u003c/em\u003e it to the store.\nTo specify how state gets updated in response to an action, you write pure \u003cem\u003ereducer\u003c/em\u003e functions that calculate a new state based on the old state and the action.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp dir=\"auto\"\u003eRedux Toolkit simplifies the process of writing Redux logic and setting up the store. With Redux Toolkit, the basic app logic looks like:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cdiv class=\"highlight highlight-source-js notranslate position-relative overflow-auto\" dir=\"auto\" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content=\"import { createSlice, configureStore } from '@reduxjs/toolkit'\n\nconst counterSlice = createSlice({\n  name: 'counter',\n  initialState: {\n    value: 0\n  },\n  reducers: {\n    incremented: state =\u0026gt; {\n      // Redux Toolkit allows us to write \u0026quot;mutating\u0026quot; logic in reducers. It\n      // doesn't actually mutate the state because it uses the Immer library,\n      // which detects changes to a \u0026quot;draft state\u0026quot; and produces a brand new\n      // immutable state based off those changes\n      state.value += 1\n    },\n    decremented: state =\u0026gt; {\n      state.value -= 1\n    }\n  }\n})\n\nexport const { incremented, decremented } = counterSlice.actions\n\nconst store = configureStore({\n  reducer: counterSlice.reducer\n})\n\n// Can still subscribe to the store\nstore.subscribe(() =\u0026gt; console.log(store.getState()))\n\n// Still pass action objects to `dispatch`, but they're created for us\nstore.dispatch(incremented())\n// {value: 1}\nstore.dispatch(incremented())\n// {value: 2}\nstore.dispatch(decremented())\n// {value: 1}\"\u003e\u003cpre\u003e\u003cspan class=\"pl-k\"\u003eimport\u003c/span\u003e \u003cspan class=\"pl-kos\"\u003e{\u003c/span\u003e \u003cspan 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">Redux is a JS library for predictable and maintainable global state management.</p>
<p dir="auto">It helps you write applications that behave consistently, run in different environments (client, server, and native), and are easy to test. On top of that, it provides a great developer experience, such as <a href="https://github.com/reduxjs/redux-devtools">live code editing combined with a time traveling debugger</a>.</p>
<p dir="auto">You can use Redux together with <a href="https://react.dev" rel="nofollow">React</a>, or with any other view library. The Redux core is tiny (2kB, including dependencies), and has a rich ecosystem of addons.</p>
<p dir="auto"><a href="https://redux-toolkit.js.org" rel="nofollow"><strong>Redux Toolkit</strong></a> is our official recommended approach for writing Redux logic. It wraps around the Redux core, and contains packages and functions that we think are essential for building a Redux app. Redux Toolkit builds in our suggested best practices, simplifies most Redux tasks, prevents common mistakes, and makes it easier to write Redux applications.</p>
<p dir="auto"><a target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer nofollow" href="https://camo.githubusercontent.com/ed3993130132d3d96177d330b2e2632e94b7792743719d8701472405cae53a41/68747470733a2f2f696d672e736869656c64732e696f2f6769746875622f616374696f6e732f776f726b666c6f772f7374617475732f72656475786a732f72656475782f746573742e79616d6c3f6272616e63683d6d6173746572266576656e743d70757368267374796c653d666c61742d737175617265"><img src="https://camo.githubusercontent.com/ed3993130132d3d96177d330b2e2632e94b7792743719d8701472405cae53a41/68747470733a2f2f696d672e736869656c64732e696f2f6769746875622f616374696f6e732f776f726b666c6f772f7374617475732f72656475786a732f72656475782f746573742e79616d6c3f6272616e63683d6d6173746572266576656e743d70757368267374796c653d666c61742d737175617265" alt="GitHub Workflow Status" data-canonical-src="https://img.shields.io/github/actions/workflow/status/reduxjs/redux/test.yaml?branch=master&amp;event=push&amp;style=flat-square" style="max-width: 100%;"></a>

<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h2 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Installation</h2><a id="user-content-installation" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Installation" href="#installation"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h3 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Create a React Redux App</h3><a id="user-content-create-a-react-redux-app" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Create a React Redux App" href="#create-a-react-redux-app"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">The recommended way to start new apps with React and Redux Toolkit is by using <a href="https://github.com/reduxjs/redux-templates">our official Redux Toolkit + TS template for Vite</a>, or by creating a new Next.js project using <a href="https://github.com/vercel/next.js/tree/canary/examples/with-redux">Next's <code>with-redux</code> template</a>.</p>
<p dir="auto">Both of these already have Redux Toolkit and React-Redux configured appropriately for that build tool, and come with a small example app that demonstrates how to use several of Redux Toolkit's features.</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-shell notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" dir="auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="# Vite with our Redux+TS template
# (using the `degit` tool to clone and extract the template)
npx degit reduxjs/redux-templates/packages/vite-template-redux my-app

# Next.js using the `with-redux` template
npx create-next-app --example with-redux my-app"><pre><span class="pl-c"><span class="pl-c">#</span> Vite with our Redux+TS template</span>
<span class="pl-c"><span class="pl-c">#</span> (using the `degit` tool to clone and extract the template)</span>
npx degit reduxjs/redux-templates/packages/vite-template-redux my-app

<span class="pl-c"><span class="pl-c">#</span> Next.js using the `with-redux` template</span>
npx create-next-app --example with-redux my-app</pre></div>
<p dir="auto">We do not currently have official React Native templates, but recommend these templates for standard React Native and for Expo:</p>
<ul dir="auto">
<li><a href="https://github.com/rahsheen/react-native-template-redux-typescript">https://github.com/rahsheen/react-native-template-redux-typescript</a></li>
<li><a href="https://github.com/rahsheen/expo-template-redux-typescript">https://github.com/rahsheen/expo-template-redux-typescript</a></li>
</ul>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="npm install @reduxjs/toolkit react-redux"><pre class="notranslate"><code>npm install @reduxjs/toolkit react-redux
</code></pre></div>
<p dir="auto">For the Redux core library by itself:</p>
<div class="snippet-clipboard-content notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="npm install redux"><pre class="notranslate"><code>npm install redux
</code></pre></div>
<p dir="auto">For more details, see <a href="https://redux.js.org/introduction/installation" rel="nofollow">the Installation docs page</a>.</p>

<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h3 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Redux Essentials Tutorial</h3><a id="user-content-redux-essentials-tutorial" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Redux Essentials Tutorial" href="#redux-essentials-tutorial"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">The <a href="https://redux.js.org/tutorials/essentials/part-1-overview-concepts" rel="nofollow"><strong>Redux Essentials tutorial</strong></a> is a "top-down" tutorial that teaches "how to use Redux the right way", using our latest recommended APIs and best practices. We recommend starting there.</p>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h3 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Redux Fundamentals Tutorial</h3><a id="user-content-redux-fundamentals-tutorial" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Redux Fundamentals Tutorial" href="#redux-fundamentals-tutorial"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">The <a href="https://redux.js.org/tutorials/fundamentals/part-1-overview" rel="nofollow"><strong>Redux Fundamentals tutorial</strong></a> is a "bottom-up" tutorial that teaches "how Redux works" from first principles and without any abstractions, and why standard Redux usage patterns exist.</p>

Reactiflux is a great place to hang out, ask questions, and learn - please come and join us there!</p>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h2 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Before Proceeding Further</h2><a id="user-content-before-proceeding-further" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Before Proceeding Further" href="#before-proceeding-further"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">Redux is a valuable tool for organizing your state, but you should also consider whether it's appropriate for your situation. Please don't use Redux just because someone said you should - instead, please take some time to understand the potential benefits and tradeoffs of using it.</p>
<p dir="auto">Here are some suggestions on when it makes sense to use Redux:</p>
<ul dir="auto">
<li>You have reasonable amounts of data changing over time</li>
<li>You need a single source of truth for your state</li>
<li>You find that keeping all your state in a top-level component is no longer sufficient</li>
</ul>
<p dir="auto">Yes, these guidelines are subjective and vague, but this is for a good reason. The point at which you should integrate Redux into your application is different for every user and different for every application.</p>
<blockquote>
<p dir="auto"><strong>For more thoughts on how Redux is meant to be used, please see:</strong><br></p>
<ul dir="auto">
<li><strong><a href="https://changelog.com/posts/when-and-when-not-to-reach-for-redux" rel="nofollow">When (and when not) to reach for Redux</a></strong></li>
<li><strong><a href="https://medium.com/@dan_abramov/you-might-not-need-redux-be46360cf367" rel="nofollow">You Might Not Need Redux</a></strong><br></li>
<li><strong><a href="https://blog.isquaredsoftware.com/2017/05/idiomatic-redux-tao-of-redux-part-1/" rel="nofollow">The Tao of Redux, Part 1 - Implementation and Intent</a></strong><br></li>
<li><strong><a href="https://blog.isquaredsoftware.com/2017/05/idiomatic-redux-tao-of-redux-part-2/" rel="nofollow">The Tao of Redux, Part 2 - Practice and Philosophy</a></strong></li>
<li><strong><a href="https://redux.js.org/faq" rel="nofollow">Redux FAQ</a></strong></li>
</ul>
</blockquote>
<div class="markdown-heading" dir="auto"><h2 tabindex="-1" class="heading-element" dir="auto">Basic Example</h2><a id="user-content-basic-example" class="anchor" aria-label="Permalink: Basic Example" href="#basic-example"><svg data-component="Octicon" class="octicon octicon-link" viewBox="0 0 16 16" version="1.1" width="16" height="16" aria-hidden="true"><path d="m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z"></path></svg></a></div>
<p dir="auto">The whole global state of your app is stored in an object tree inside a single <em>store</em>.
The only way to change the state tree is to create an <em>action</em>, an object describing what happened, and <em>dispatch</em> it to the store.
To specify how state gets updated in response to an action, you write pure <em>reducer</em> functions that calculate a new state based on the old state and the action.</p>
<p dir="auto">Redux Toolkit simplifies the process of writing Redux logic and setting up the store. With Redux Toolkit, the basic app logic looks like:</p>
<div class="highlight highlight-source-js notranslate position-relative overflow-auto" dir="auto" data-snippet-clipboard-copy-content="import { createSlice, configureStore } from '@reduxjs/toolkit'

const counterSlice = createSlice({
  name: 'counter',
  initialState: {
    value: 0
  },
  reducers: {
    incremented: state =&gt; {
      // Redux Toolkit allows us to write &quot;mutating&quot; logic in reducers. It
      // doesn't actually mutate the state because it uses the Immer library,
      // which detects changes to a &quot;draft state&quot; and produces a brand new
      // immutable state based off those changes
      state.value += 1
    },
    decremented: state =&gt; {
      state.value -= 1
    }
  }
})

export const { incremented, decremented } = counterSlice.actions

const store = configureStore({
  reducer: counterSlice.reducer
})

// Can still subscribe to the store
store.subscribe(() =&gt; console.log(store.getState()))

// Still pass action objects to `dispatch`, but they're created for us
store.dispatch(incremented())
// {value: 1}
store.dispatch(incremented())
// {value: 2}
store.dispatch(decremented())
// {value: 1}"><pre><span class="pl-k">import</span> <span class="pl-kos">{</span> <span class="pl-s1">createSlice</span><span class="pl-kos">,</span> <span class="pl-s1">configureStore</span> <span class="pl-kos">}</span> <span class="pl-k">from</span> <span class="pl-s">'@reduxjs/toolkit'</span>

<span class="pl-k">const</span> <span class="pl-s1">counterSlice</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-en">createSlice</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-kos">{</span>
  <span class="pl-c1">name</span>: <span class="pl-s">'counter'</span><span class="pl-kos">,</span>
  <span class="pl-c1">initialState</span>: <span class="pl-kos">{</span>
    <span class="pl-c1">value</span>: <span class="pl-c1">0</span>
  <span class="pl-kos">}</span><span class="pl-kos">,</span>
  <span class="pl-c1">reducers</span>: <span class="pl-kos">{</span>
    <span class="pl-en">incremented</span>: <span class="pl-s1">state</span> <span class="pl-c1">=&gt;</span> <span class="pl-kos">{</span>
      <span class="pl-c">// Redux Toolkit allows us to write "mutating" logic in reducers. It</span>
      <span class="pl-c">// doesn't actually mutate the state because it uses the Immer library,</span>
      <span class="pl-c">// which detects changes to a "draft state" and produces a brand new</span>
      <span class="pl-c">// immutable state based off those changes</span>
      <span class="pl-s1">state</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-c1">value</span> <span class="pl-c1">+=</span> <span class="pl-c1">1</span>
    <span class="pl-kos">}</span><span class="pl-kos">,</span>
    <span class="pl-en">decremented</span>: <span class="pl-s1">state</span> <span class="pl-c1">=&gt;</span> <span class="pl-kos">{</span>
      <span class="pl-s1">state</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-c1">value</span> <span class="pl-c1">-=</span> <span class="pl-c1">1</span>
    <span class="pl-kos">}</span>
  <span class="pl-kos">}</span>
<span class="pl-kos">}</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span>

<span class="pl-k">export</span> <span class="pl-k">const</span> <span class="pl-kos">{</span> incremented<span class="pl-kos">,</span> decremented <span class="pl-kos">}</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-s1">counterSlice</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-c1">actions</span>

<span class="pl-k">const</span> <span class="pl-s1">store</span> <span class="pl-c1">=</span> <span class="pl-en">configureStore</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-kos">{</span>
  <span class="pl-c1">reducer</span>: <span class="pl-s1">counterSlice</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-c1">reducer</span>
<span class="pl-kos">}</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span>

<span class="pl-c">// Can still subscribe to the store</span>
<span class="pl-s1">store</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">subscribe</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span> <span class="pl-c1">=&gt;</span> <span class="pl-smi">console</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">log</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-s1">store</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">getState</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span>

<span class="pl-c">// Still pass action objects to `dispatch`, but they're created for us</span>
<span class="pl-s1">store</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">dispatch</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-en">incremented</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span>
<span class="pl-c">// {value: 1}</span>
<span class="pl-s1">store</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">dispatch</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-en">incremented</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span>
<span class="pl-c">// {value: 2}</span>
<span class="pl-s1">store</span><span class="pl-kos">.</span><span class="pl-en">dispatch</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-en">decremented</span><span class="pl-kos">(</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span><span class="pl-kos">)</span>
<span class="pl-c">// {value: 1}</span></pre></div>
<p dir="auto">Redux Toolkit allows us to write shorter logic that's easier to read, while still following the original core Redux behavior and data flow.</p>
